FC01 — Dummit–Foote mapping

Status: Sweep II complete for FC01. All 1,234 stable catalogue IDs have one explicit route verdict.

Provenance contract

  • Project search: repository sources outside .lake, .agents, and .git; no project-owned replacement for the C00 substrate notions was found. Existing project uses are not counted as owned formalizations.
  • Pinned Mathlib: C00–C05 declaration types were checked with lake env lean at .lake/packages/mathlib@915159e569989ee5b74673bb4a4ddaa022e7b356. Before the C06 audit the repository pin advanced to Mathlib v4.32.0, commit 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; C06 declaration types were checked against that pinned checkout.
  • Current Mathlib: leanprover-community/mathlib4 current-default-branch search/fetch on 2026-09-05. The C00/C01 search exposed commit fe6e3cde435e82b2407df2760cbac392694c9e64; the C02–C04 refresh exposed 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8 as the latest inspected default-branch commit. The relevant C02–C04 interfaces below were rechecked against that current tree.
  • exact match means the same assertion/interface up to harmless packaging or notation; broad match means the concept exists, often more generally, but not through the exact textbook interface; partial match / wrong interface means a substantive clause/interface is missing; unmatched after search is reserved for an exhausted search.

C00 — Preliminaries

C01 — Introduction to Groups

Project search found no project-owned C01 replacement: the repository imports/uses Mathlib group structures, but does not redeclare the chapter’s elementary group theory. Pinned declaration checks used the group, permutation, presented-group, finite-field, general-linear-group, and action modules; current-upstream searches confirm the same principal interfaces (DihedralGroup, cycle-factor decomposition, MulAction.toPermHom, and Cayley’s theorem). The only external-package verdicts below are called out explicitly.

FC01 ID Verdict Lean target(s) Provenance Interface comparison
FC01-C01-U001 broad match Mul α; raw operation α → α → α Lean core / pinned Mathlib algebra hierarchy A binary operation is native data; Mul bundles the multiplicative notation but imposes no axioms.
FC01-C01-U002 exact match Group G Lean core / Mathlib algebra hierarchy Exact group structure: multiplication, identity, inverse, associativity and inverse laws.
FC01-C01-U003 exact match additive-group instances on Int, Rat, Real, Complex Lean core + Mathlib numeric instances Exact standard additive groups.
FC01-C01-U004 exact match product Group (A × B) / Prod.instGroup Mathlib product instances Componentwise multiplication, identity and inverse give the exact direct-product group.
FC01-C01-U005 exact match group axioms plus inv_inv, mul_inv_rev, identity/inverse uniqueness lemmas Mathlib group core All listed basic identities are direct consequences already exposed in the generic group API.
FC01-C01-U006 broad match multiplicative Group / additive AddGroup hierarchies and notation Lean core / Mathlib algebra hierarchy Mathlib formalizes the two notational conventions as parallel typeclass hierarchies rather than one prose convention.
FC01-C01-U007 exact match mul_left_cancel, mul_right_cancel, inverse-multiplication equivalences Mathlib group core Exact unique-solvability/cancellation content for equations in a group.
FC01-C01-U008 broad match orderOf : G → ℕ Mathlib/GroupTheory/OrderOfElement.lean Same order invariant, but Mathlib represents infinite order by 0 instead of a separate symbol such as ∞.
FC01-C01-U009 broad match finite group multiplication function plus Fintype enumeration group/Fintype core A multiplication table is derivable data for a finite group; Mathlib has no separate multiplication-table object matching the textbook display convention.
FC01-C01-U010 exact match DihedralGroup n; DihedralGroup.card, DihedralGroup.nat_card pinned/current Mathlib/GroupTheory/SpecificGroups/Dihedral.lean Exact dihedral group of order 2*n for positive finite n.
FC01-C01-U011 exact match DihedralGroup.r, DihedralGroup.sr, r_one_pow_n, sr_mul_self, r_mul_sr, sr_mul_r pinned/current dihedral file The rotation/reflection normal forms and defining multiplication relations are exposed directly.
FC01-C01-U012 exact match Subgroup.closure S; criterion Subgroup.closure S = ⊤ Mathlib subgroup API Exact notion of a subset generating a group.
FC01-C01-U013 broad match relators in FreeGroup α; PresentedGroup relation sets Mathlib/GroupTheory/PresentedGroup.lean A relation among generators is represented as a relator word in the free group; this is a more structural interface than the informal equation notation.
FC01-C01-U014 exact match PresentedGroup rels pinned/current Mathlib/GroupTheory/PresentedGroup.lean Exact generators-and-relations quotient construction.
FC01-C01-U015 broad match DihedralGroup n together with PresentedGroup and the dihedral defining relations pinned/current dihedral + presented-group files Both sides of the textbook dihedral presentation are formalized, but no pinned/current public declaration identifying that specific presentation with DihedralGroup n was found.
FC01-C01-U016 broad match PresentedGroup; quotient of FreeGroup by relation closure pinned/current presented-group API The collapse phenomena in the examples are expressible through the presentation quotient, but the individual textbook example calculations are not named declarations.
FC01-C01-U017 exact match Equiv.Perm Ω; Fintype.card_perm pinned/current permutation API Exact symmetric group on a type; for finite Ω, cardinality is the factorial of Fintype.card Ω.
FC01-C01-U018 exact match Equiv.Perm.IsCycle; cycleOf; concrete-cycle/list API Mathlib/GroupTheory/Perm/Cycle/* Exact notion of a permutation cycle, with structural rather than parenthesized-cycle representation.
FC01-C01-U019 exact match Equiv.Perm.cycleFactorsFinset pinned/current Mathlib/GroupTheory/Perm/Cycle/Factors.lean Gives the canonical finite set of disjoint nontrivial cycle factors of a finite-support permutation.
FC01-C01-U020 exact match Equiv.Perm.Disjoint; disjoint-support characterizations pinned/current permutation-support API Exact disjointness notion for permutations/cycles.
FC01-C01-U021 broad match multiplication/composition on Equiv.Perm Lean/Mathlib permutation group interface Function-composition order is fixed by the library’s multiplication convention; the textbook “right-to-left” instruction is a notation convention, not a separate theorem.
FC01-C01-U022 exact match Equiv.Perm.Disjoint.commute pinned/current Mathlib/GroupTheory/Perm/Support.lean Exact theorem that disjoint permutations, hence disjoint cycles, commute.
FC01-C01-U023 exact match Equiv.Perm.toList_pow_apply_eq_rotate; SameCycle.toList_isRotated pinned Mathlib/GroupTheory/Perm/Cycle/Concrete.lean; current cycle API Exact invariance of a cycle under cyclic rotation, expressed through the canonical list representation.
FC01-C01-U024 exact match cycleFactorsFinset_eq_finset; cycleFactorsFinset_injective pinned/current cycle-factors file Exact existence/uniqueness characterization of the disjoint-cycle factorization.
FC01-C01-U025 exact match Equiv.Perm.lcm_cycleType pinned/current Mathlib/GroupTheory/Perm/Cycle/Type.lean Exact formula: the lcm of cycle lengths is orderOf σ.
FC01-C01-U026 broad match finite field as [Field F] [Fintype F] / FiniteField namespace pinned/current finite-field API Mathlib uses typeclasses rather than a special notation object F_q; finite-field cardinality is tracked separately.
FC01-C01-U027 exact match unit group Fˣ for a field F Mathlib units/group-with-zero API In a field, the multiplicative group of nonzero elements is exactly the unit group.
FC01-C01-U028 exact match Matrix.GeneralLinearGroup n F / notation GL pinned/current general-linear-group API Exact group of invertible matrices.
FC01-C01-U029 exact match FiniteField.card, FiniteField.card', FiniteField.isPrimePow_card pinned/current Mathlib/FieldTheory/Finite/Basic.lean Exact theorem that the cardinality of a finite field is a positive power of its prime characteristic.

C02 — Subgroups

Project search found no project-owned replacement for the elementary subgroup theory in C02. Pinned declaration checks were compiled successfully at 915159e569989ee5b74673bb4a4ddaa022e7b356 using explicit subgroup, action, cyclic, dihedral, permutation-closure, Klein-four, and order-cover imports. Current-upstream searches at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8 retain the principal interfaces used below; a broad open-PR search found no C02-specific replacement or stronger pending interface.

External search included the direct Dummit–Foote formalization jhanschoo/texts_lean@7a6ccd2f6b306cf4fbebe66fd2f218c6af4220ed (MIT). Its C02 files are useful statement-level cross-checks, but the two geometric action examples in §2.2 are explicitly TODO, many §2.3/§2.4 theorem statements are admitted with sorry, and §2.5 is entirely TODO; those are not counted as reusable proved declarations. Targeted searches of the source atlas, atlas-lean, and broader GitHub Lean repositories found no exact formalization of the D8 vertex/opposite-pair actions, the listed complete small-group subgroup lattices, or the D16 join/intersection calculation. Qiuzhen-CFSG/CFSG@96b2a02085dc678f3e0a97b334c31ada599c55fd (Apache-2.0) does provide reusable even-dihedral reflection-centralizer infrastructure, recorded at U040.

C03 — Quotients, Isomorphism Theorems, Composition Series, and Alternating Groups

Project search again found no project-owned elementary group-theory replacement. A pinned declaration audit compiled successfully at 915159e569989ee5b74673bb4a4ddaa022e7b356 with explicit kernel, coset/index, quotient, Sylow, group-extension, solvable, cyclic, alternating, sign/cycle-type, and Jordan–Hölder imports. Current-upstream inspection at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8 retains the principal interfaces used below and still contains the Mathlib TODO for a subgroup JordanHolderLattice instance. Loogle, LeanSearch, Reservoir, source-atlas, current/open GitHub, and broad GitHub Lean searches were included for the concrete-example and theorem gaps.

External source checks: jhanschoo/texts_lean@7a6ccd2f6b306cf4fbebe66fd2f218c6af4220ed (MIT; https://github.com/jhanschoo/texts_lean) directly tracks Dummit–Foote C03 but leaves several §3.2–§3.4 results admitted/TODO and has no C03.5 file. yawara/odd-order@82e8b66fe80a2eb9f0c6568bf288186086af73af (Apache-2.0; https://github.com/yawara/odd-order) supplies proved Feit–Thompson and Hall-theorem infrastructure used at U043/U046. rjwalters/lean-genius@f9c62750e76180f15c7bd6c5759be320d7feffdc contains a 0-sorry subgroup Jordan–Hölder instance and uniqueness theorem, but no repository license was found in the inspected tree, so it is search evidence only and is not classified reference-port. The searched TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap@64b1d0240921750aa46a898269ed40cd42beda4c CFSG material is statement/roadmap scaffolding, not a proof.

C07 — Introduction to Rings

Project search found no project-owned C07 replacement. The declaration audit was compiled against the repository’s pinned Mathlib v4.32.0 checkout at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997, with explicit imports for non-unital rings/subrings, two-sided ideals and ring congruences, quaternions, quadratic/Pell/number-field units, polynomials, matrices, monoid algebras, ideals, localization, rational functions, CRT, and units of products.

The source convention that a ring need not have an identity is material here. Accordingly, NonUnitalRing, NonUnitalCommRing, NonUnitalRingHom, NonUnitalRingEquiv, NonUnitalSubring, and TwoSidedIdeal are used for the source’s base notions; Mathlib Ring, RingHom, and ordinary commutative Ideal are not silently substituted where that would strengthen the source interface.

External search found one directly reusable modern source for the group-ring rows: TauCetiProject/TauCeti@d6214142a5e6403ba4f977246f1fc16418d561e4, TauCeti/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/Exactness.lean, Apache-2.0, defines TauCeti.MonoidAlgebra.augmentation and proves ker_augmentation_eq_span. A legacy Lean-3 source, lean-forward/class-group-and-mordell-equation@baba2049f3bfe4d2cc184f8205997333e7c58638, contains the exact quadratic-ring-of-integers split in src/number_theory/quad_ring/basic.lean, but no repository license could be verified and the interface is Lean-3-era, so it is evidence only and not a reusable route. No searched licensed current Lean source supplied the integral-quaternion subring example, the full imaginary-quadratic unit lists, the explicit full unit group of ℤ[√2], the fixed-row/fixed-column one-sided matrix ideals, or the nonprincipality of (2,X) ⊂ ℤ[X].

FC01 ID Verdict Route Lean target(s) Provenance Interface comparison
FC01-C07-U001 exact match mathlib NonUnitalRing; NonUnitalCommRing; Ring pinned/current algebra hierarchy Mathlib splits the source definition exactly: NonUnitalRing is the base ring, NonUnitalCommRing adds commutativity, and Ring adds an identity.
FC01-C07-U002 exact match mathlib additive/multiplicative notation for ring structures pinned/current algebra core Juxtaposition, 0, and unary negation are the standard operations of the corresponding ring structures.
FC01-C07-U003 exact match mathlib DivisionRing; Field pinned/current field hierarchy These are exactly the source notions: a nontrivial unital ring with inverses for nonzero elements, with Field the commutative specialization.
FC01-C07-U004 broad match mathlib NonUnitalRing structure construction; subsingleton/zero-ring instances pinned/current algebra core Zero multiplication on an arbitrary additive commutative group is immediately expressible by a structure instance and the one-element specialization is the zero ring, but no searched named constructor packages this textbook example.
FC01-C07-U005 exact match mathlib Quaternion ℝ; quaternion basis/multiplication; Quaternion.normSq; division-ring instance pinned/current Mathlib/Algebra/Quaternion.lean Mathlib’s real quaternions have the displayed basis relations, norm-square, conjugation/inverse formula, and a division-ring instance; this is the source construction rather than an abstract surrogate.
FC01-C07-U006 exact match mathlib Pi/product non-unital/ring instances pinned/current algebra Pi/product instances Pointwise addition and multiplication on X → A give exactly the function ring, with commutativity and identity inherited pointwise from A.

C08 — Euclidean Domains, Principal Ideal Domains, and Unique Factorization Domains

Project search found no project-owned C08 replacement. The local declaration audit was compiled against the repository’s pinned Mathlib v4.32.0 checkout at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997, with explicit Euclidean-domain, polynomial, Gaussian-integer, discrete-valuation-ring, principal-ideal, unique-factorization, Dedekind-domain, class-group, and sum-of-two-squares interfaces.

Two external Lean 4 developments close several textbook-example gaps. franv314/non-euclidean-pid@087a99dc84ea7f0f70f614556f02111df63dd103 (MIT; no searched sorry) formalizes the ring ℤ[(1+√-19)/2], a universal-side-divisor obstruction to Euclideanity, a Dedekind–Hasse-style descent relation, and the resulting PID/non-Euclidean example; it pins an older Mathlib (31af98b7…), so it is recorded as a reference-port rather than a direct package import. WangFrankie/QuadraticNumberFields@8943a27707b9b6059811b6f741ea84957ae1be25 (Apache-2.0; Mathlib v4.30.0-rc2) contains sorry-free concrete files Examples/SqrtNeg5/Ideals.lean and Examples/SqrtNeg5/ClassNumber.lean proving the standard ℤ[√-5] ideal factorizations, a nonprincipal prime ideal, its principal square, and class number two. That repository also contains explicitly placeholder files elsewhere; none of those admitted declarations are counted here.

FC01 ID Verdict Route Lean target(s) Provenance Interface comparison
FC01-C08-U001 broad match mathlib ordinary functions R → ℕ/R → ℤ plus positivity predicates pinned/current core arithmetic The source’s “norm” is only data—a nonnegative integer-valued function vanishing at zero—so it is directly expressible. Mathlib does not bundle this particular weak notion under a dedicated Euclidean-domain norm type.
FC01-C08-U002 partial match / wrong interface mathlib EuclideanDomain; EuclideanDomain.quotient; EuclideanDomain.remainder; EuclideanDomain.remainder_lt pinned/current Mathlib/Algebra/EuclideanDomain Mathlib packages exactly the division algorithm and termination, but uses an arbitrary well-founded remainder relation rather than requiring existence of a ℤ_{≥0}-valued norm. The mathematical capability is stronger; the defining interface differs.
FC01-C08-U003 exact match mathlib EuclideanDomain.quotient; EuclideanDomain.remainder; quotient_mul_add_remainder_eq pinned/current Euclidean-domain API Quotient and remainder are first-class operations satisfying the source decomposition.

Termination is by the generalized well-founded remainder relation rather than a numeric norm.
FC01-C08-U005 exact match mathlib Field.toEuclideanDomain pinned/current Algebra/EuclideanDomain/Field.lean Every field receives the Euclidean-domain structure directly, with zero remainder division.

FC01-C08-U009 broad match mathlib Valuation; Valuation.IsRankOneDiscrete; integer/value-group valuation APIs pinned/current valuation APIs Mathlib’s valuation interface is multiplicative and value-group based rather than literally a surjective additive homomorphism Kˣ → ℤ, but rank-one discrete valuations encode the same discrete valuation data after the standard order/group equivalence.

C09 — Polynomial Rings

Project search found one C09-specific owner: LeanCategories/Algebra/Polynomial.lean defines the leading-term ideal and a finite IsGroebnerBasis predicate, adapted under Apache-2.0 from WuProver. The ordinary polynomial, multivariate-polynomial, quotient, irreducibility, Noetherian, root, monomial-order, and ideal operations are otherwise supplied by Mathlib rather than duplicated locally.

The pinned declaration audit uses Mathlib v4.32.0 at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; current upstream master was checked on 2026-09-05 at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8. The targeted current/pinned search covered polynomial and multivariate polynomial quotients, Euclidean division, Gauss lemma/UFD transfer, irreducibility criteria, roots, cyclic unit groups, Noetherian polynomial rings, monomial orders, and the current multivariate Gröbner primitives. google-deepmind/formal-conjectures@230782e37bbf5aa0cab03874e876626beacac009 and the local reference corpus were also searched for the concrete examples and missing Gröbner/elimination statements.

For the Gröbner-theory tranche, WuProver/groebner_proj@42910339be485d279407382576e767223898c543 (Apache-2.0) proves finite Gröbner-basis existence, unique remainders, Buchberger’s criterion, minimal/reduced bases, reduction of a minimal basis, and uniqueness of the reduced basis. That revision targets Lean v4.29.0-rc8, so it is recorded as a reference-port, not a direct package import into this v4.32.0 repository. Its current WIP head 96a41217cef0acb131780e0103c037367f362857 and WuProver/GroebnerTactic@1cbbc7122e52e757ba6502f450a3cfd1df243b26 were checked separately: the latter delegates Gröbner computation to SageMath/SymPy and kernel-checks certificates, but neither searched development supplies a formal Buchberger iteration/termination theorem or an elimination theorem.

C10 — Introduction to Module Theory

Project search found no custom owner that supersedes the standard module interfaces: LeanCategories/Modules/Mathlib.lean, Framed.lean, and Presented.lean wrap or refine Mathlib’s ModuleCat, free-module, projective, and presentation APIs rather than replacing the underlying theory. The pinned declaration audit uses Mathlib v4.32.0 at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; current upstream master was checked on 2026-09-05 at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8 for the material hypothesis boundaries.

Two source/interface boundaries control the verdicts. First, Dummit–Foote U001 explicitly permits a base ring without 1, while Mathlib’s ordinary Module starts from a unital Semiring/Ring; the later ordinary module theory is therefore matched only after the source imposes the usual unital convention. Second, §§10.4–10.5 deliberately develop tensor products and flatness for right/left modules over arbitrary noncommutative rings. Pinned and current Mathlib’s ordinary TensorProduct still has a commutative base-ring interface, and RingTheory/Flat/Basic.lean explicitly records noncommutative flatness as future generalization. The commutative specializations map strongly; the genuinely balanced right/left/bimodule statements are recorded as interface gaps rather than silently commutativized.

The module-theoretic Hom/projective/injective side has a different boundary. Mathlib’s projective lifting/direct-summand equivalences, injective extension property, Baer criterion, and enough-projectives/enough-injectives infrastructure are available over general module categories. Its dedicated linear-map theorem LinearAlgebra.LeftExact is phrased over a commutative ring because the Hom sets are themselves treated as modules; Dummit–Foote’s U065/U071 statements only need exactness of the underlying abelian groups over an arbitrary ring, so those rows use the general ModuleCat/abelian-category interface where possible and do not infer a commutativity hypothesis into the source.

The source corollary occurs in the general noncommutative right-module setting.
FC01-C10-U083 partial match / wrong interface mathlib TensorProduct.curry; TensorProduct.uncurry; AlgebraTensorModule.curry; ModuleCat monoidal closed structure pinned/current tensor-Hom API Tensor–Hom adjunction/currying is directly formalized for commutative bases and algebra towers. The source theorem allows arbitrary rings with a right module and an (R,S)-bimodule, which the ordinary tensor interface does not express.
FC01-C10-U084 exact match mathlib Module.Projective.tensorProduct pinned/current Algebra/Module/Projective.lean Under the source’s explicit commutativity hypothesis, the tensor product of projective modules has a direct projective instance.

C11 — Vector Spaces

Project search found no C11-specific replacement for the standard linear-algebra owners. LeanCategories/Algebra/FreeAlgebras.lean wraps Mathlib’s symmetric/exterior algebra constructions functorially, but the declaration-level mathematics in this chapter is primarily Mathlib. The pinned audit uses Mathlib v4.32.0 at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; current upstream master was checked on 2026-09-05 at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8 where a missing bridge could plausibly have appeared.

The first four sections map unusually closely: basis extension, cardinal/finite dimension, matrix representation and Kronecker products, algebraic duality including the infinite-dimensional Erdős–Kaplansky inequality, and determinant/cofactor/adjugate theory all have direct library owners. The main interface gaps occur in §11.5. Mathlib separately formalizes tensor algebras, symmetric algebras, symmetric tensor powers, exterior algebras, exterior powers, permutation reindexing, and alternatization. It does not currently expose a generic quotient-grading theorem matching U054, nor the textbook identification, when k! is invertible, of symmetric/exterior quotient powers with the fixed/sign submodules inside the tensor power. Those missing bridges are not inferred from the adjacent APIs.

C12 — Modules over Principal Ideal Domains

Project search found two C12-specific owners. LeanCategories/Algebra/CanonicalForms.lean defines the Dummit–Foote companion-matrix convention, ordered divisibility-chain InvariantFactorData, and the associated block-diagonal rational-canonical matrix. LeanCategories/Algebra/JordanCanonical.lean defines Jordan blocks, block data, matrix similarity, and Jordan-form predicates; its module documentation explicitly records that it proves no existence, uniqueness, or classification theorem. The pinned audit uses Mathlib v4.32.0 at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; the recorded current-upstream audit and a fresh 2026-09-05 GitHub search were also checked for canonical-form or invariant-factor bridges.

The PID infrastructure is stronger than a name search suggests. Mathlib directly proves Noetherianity of PIDs, finite torsion-free PID modules are free, Smith diagonal bases for submodules of finite free modules, elementary prime-power decomposition of finitely generated PID modules, and primary decomposition for torsion modules. The decisive mismatch is normalization and uniqueness: Module.Basis.SmithNormalForm stores diagonal coefficients but no divisibility chain, and Module.equiv_free_prod_directSum is an elementary-divisor existence theorem rather than the source’s unique invariant-factor classification. The same boundary propagates into rational and Jordan canonical forms: eigenvalue, determinant, characteristic/minimal-polynomial, matrix-operation, and semisimplicity APIs are substantial, but no searched declaration supplies the rational- or Jordan-canonical existence/uniqueness theorems. Mathlib’s LinearAlgebra/Semisimple.lean still lists the diagonalizability bridge as a TODO, so squarefree-minimal-polynomial results are not promoted to the source diagonalization criterion.

C13 — Field Theory

Project search found one C13-specific file, LeanCategories/Algebra/Concrete/FieldExtensions.lean, but it is categorical wrapping only: its module documentation says the carrier predicates are Mathlib’s standard field-extension predicates and that the file introduces no field-extension data. The mathematical mappings therefore remain in Mathlib unless an external implementation supplies a missing interface. The pinned audit uses Mathlib v4.32.0 at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; the current default branch was rechecked on 2026-09-05 at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8.

Field-extension, minimal-polynomial, finite-generation, compositum, splitting-field, algebraic-closure, separability, finite-field, and cyclotomic infrastructure is already extensive. The main pinned-Mathlib gaps are narrower: no searched theorem classifies every characteristic-not-two quadratic extension as F(√D), and no searched theorem gives the textbook splitting-field bound [E:F] ≤ n!. Mathlib’s own KummerExtension.lean still records the missing degree-two Kummer/quadratic bridge.

For §13.3, a fresh external search found wwylele/Compass (https://github.com/wwylele/Compass), Apache-2.0, commit c1c3df7fead49c56dab02dc6cedebcdf8d82739d (2026-08-29). It formalizes straightedge-and-compass constructibility, constructible closure as iterated quadratic extensions, the power-of-two degree obstruction, angle-trisection impossibility, and cube-doubling impossibility. Its toolchain is Lean v4.34.0-rc1 with Mathlib commit 4ac22c7efcf253ec41e1b469f381b4ab68ca2646, so against this repository’s pinned v4.32 environment it is recorded as reference-port, not package-import. No squaring-the-circle theorem was found in that repository or the fresh external search.

C14 — Galois Theory

Project search found no C14-specific theorem layer beyond the existing categorical field-extension wrappers, so the primary route is Mathlib. The pinned audit uses Mathlib v4.32.0 at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; current upstream was rechecked at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8. Finite Galois correspondence, fixed fields, finite fields, Galois closures, Kummer cyclic extensions, transcendence bases, inseparability degrees, and infinite Galois correspondence are all substantial and are mapped declaration-by-declaration rather than by topic name.

Fresh external search also found four relevant newer Lean developments. TauCeti supplies concrete multiquadratic results for Q(sqrt 2,sqrt 3); wwylele/Compass supplies Gauss–Wantzel; xroblot/SKW proves Kronecker–Weber; and CBirkbeck/AINTLIB proves the conjugacy-class Chebotarev density theorem. Each targets a newer Lean/Mathlib revision than this repository, so these are recorded as reference-port, not package-import. The external search did not close the source’s x^8-2/quasidihedral examples, Gaussian periods, discriminant/alternating-group criteria, quartic resolvent, converse solvability-by-radicals theorem, Hilbert irreducibility statement, or the full polynomial-factorization formulation of Chebotarev.

FC01 ID Verdict Route Lean target(s) Provenance Interface comparison
FC01-C14-U001 exact match mathlib AlgEquiv; Gal pinned Mathlib v4.32.0 Field automorphisms and base-fixing automorphisms are direct algebra-equivalence objects.
FC01-C14-U002 exact match mathlib Gal; group structure on AlgEquiv pinned Mathlib v4.32.0 The automorphisms fixing the base form exactly the composition group used for Gal(K/F).
FC01-C14-U003 exact match mathlib Gal; Subgroup pinned Mathlib v4.32.0 The automorphism group and its subgroups are native group-theoretic objects.
FC01-C14-U004 exact match mathlib minpoly.map_root pinned Mathlib v4.32.0 An algebra embedding fixing the base sends an algebraic element to another root of its minimal polynomial.

C15 — Commutative Rings and Algebraic Geometry

Project search found two C15-owned interfaces already present: LeanCategories.Algebra.Ideal.IsPrimaryDecomposition with isolated/embedded-prime vocabulary, and AffineVarietyDimension on prime-spectrum points. The remaining direct coverage is primarily Mathlib. The pinned audit uses Mathlib v4.32.0 at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; current upstream was rechecked at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8.

The chapter crosses two distinct geometric interfaces and they are not conflated here. Mathlib has MvPolynomial.zeroLocus/vanishingIdeal and the Nullstellensatz, but it does not provide the textbook’s full elementary category of affine algebraic sets with polynomial maps and coordinate rings. By contrast, the later prime-spectrum and affine-scheme material has a substantial first-class implementation: Zariski topology, principal opens, localization, structure sheaf, stalks, and the affine-scheme/commutative-ring anti-equivalence. Fresh external search did not close the concrete quadratic-ring-of-integers formula or the Gröbner elimination/radical-membership examples.

C16 — Artinian Rings, Discrete Valuation Rings, and Dedekind Domains

Project search found an owned fractional-ideal/class-group wrapper in LeanCategories.Algebra.FractionalIdeals, while the main algebraic coverage is in Mathlib’s Artinian/Jacobson, DVR, fractional-ideal, class-group, Dedekind-domain, and module files. The pinned audit uses Mathlib v4.32.0 at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; current upstream was rechecked at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8.

The last three module-structure units require a strict interface distinction. Mathlib proves the prime-power decomposition of finitely generated torsion modules over a Dedekind domain and has enough general flat/projective machinery to derive the finitely-generated torsion-free/projective equivalence, but the searched library does not expose the full Steinitz free-plus-ideal classification or its direct-sum law.

C17 — Introduction to Homological Algebra and Group Cohomology

Project search found useful surrounding infrastructure but no project-owned replacement for the chapter’s main Ext/Tor/group-cohomology interfaces. The pinned audit uses Mathlib v4.32.0 at 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997; current upstream was rechecked at 7974e751bece493b6ff508039423ca9fa2452fa8.

Mathlib’s coverage is sharply nonuniform. Complexes, projective/injective resolutions, Ext, ordinary group cohomology, the bar resolution, low-degree H¹/H² cocycle interfaces, Shapiro, finite cyclic cohomology, Hilbert 90, and Schur–Zassenhaus have substantial first-class APIs. Tor is present as a derived functor but its own documentation still lacks the comparison between deriving tensor in the two variables and the flatness/Tor characterizations used in the source. Continuous cohomology has a first-class topological-representation construction and functoriality, while its current TODOs still include comparison with ordinary cohomology for discrete groups and a general long exact sequence. Finally, Mathlib’s group-extension file explicitly leaves the H¹-complements and H²-extension classifications unfinished, and its Brauer-group file currently defines only the quotient setoid/type while listing the tensor-product group law and field functoriality as TODOs. Fresh external search does, however, close much of the field-theoretic tail: Whysoserioushah/BrauerGroup at immutable revision fbb4a349e85f66c02b2ccccd80516eec0d5d1818 (Apache-2.0; Lean v4.34.0-rc2, Mathlib be629e7a…) constructs crossed products, develops splitting and Wedderburn theory, proves the Brauer group law and scalar-extension functoriality, defines relative Brauer groups, and proves the finite-Galois equivalence Br(K/F) ≃ H²(G,Kˣ). It is therefore recorded as a reference-port, not a package import.

C18 — Representations and characters

C18 was audited against repository HEAD’s pinned Mathlib revision 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997 (Lean v4.32.0) and rechecked against current upstream Mathlib at 69fe4f49ffbc9580a2c3ae6d29591ef6d1d4131e. The local worktree’s newer Mathlib checkout was used only as a supplementary search surface because its dependency files are unrelated dirty work. Project-source and local-mirror searches found no stronger project-owned C18 implementation.

The main external reference found in the broad Lean search is TauCetiProject/TauCeti at immutable revision d7bf8387e88bbf5f262414f5e112d480b24b2519, Apache-2.0. That revision uses Lean v4.34.0-rc2 and Mathlib 5fcc6656691ed31965746c369f41fa75e567ac9d, so its declarations are recorded as reference-port, not package-import. In particular its character-table development supplies the completeness, column orthogonality, degree-divisibility, primitive-central-idempotent, permutation-character, and character-value interfaces absent from pinned/current Mathlib.

C19 — Character theory applications

C19 was audited in canonical source order against repository HEAD’s pinned Mathlib revision 81a5d257c8e410db227a6665ed08f64fea08e997 (Lean v4.32.0), with project-source searches first and current/open external sources checked only after the pinned surface. No project-owned C19 implementation was found. The concrete character-table rows were also checked against current TauCeti; no formal S4, A4, or S5 table owner surfaced in the audited Mathlib/project/TauCeti/odd-order/local-source-atlas surfaces as of 2026-09-05.

Two external references supply most of the non-Mathlib coverage. ianklatzco/odd-order-lean at immutable revision 5cf07626edf04b2db9ea03fde77a08ec009c6feb is Apache-2.0, uses Lean v4.32.0-rc1 and Mathlib 360da6fa66c1273b76b6b2d8c5666fd5ac2e3b56, and contains the Burnside, character-arithmetic, Hall, induction, virtual-character, and Frobenius-system interfaces below. TauCetiProject/TauCeti at immutable revision d7bf8387e88bbf5f262414f5e112d480b24b2519 is Apache-2.0, uses Lean v4.34.0-rc2 and Mathlib 5fcc6656691ed31965746c369f41fa75e567ac9d, and contains the executable character tables and the newer finite-dimensional induction / exceptional-character APIs. Both are therefore recorded as reference-port, not package-import.

Appendix I — Cartesian Products and Zorn’s Lemma

Appendix I was audited in canonical source order against repository main’s current pinned Mathlib revision db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d (v4.33.0). The public Mathlib source was checked declaration-by-declaration for dependent products, cardinal products, partial orders/chains, Zorn’s lemma, classical choice, and the well-ordering theorem. The malformed catalogue text for FC01-AI-U006 was source-reconstructed as the cardinality formula for an indexed Cartesian product and repaired without changing its stable ID. The external exercise-solution source gkikola/sol-dummit-foote@354e7df324bf4ff5ce5106d881154cd09490161e was checked as a secondary source; its Appendix I file contains the reindexing exercise but is not a theorem owner.

A broad Lean search for the source’s final ZF-equivalence theorem found suii00/lean-experiments@3338840a3f5868ea9d25dd48b49c9abab01499bc, but its purported reverse implications ignore the Zorn/well-ordering hypotheses and invoke Lean’s ambient classical choice. It therefore does not formalize equivalence over ZF and is not credited as an exact or reference-port match.

Appendix II — Category Theory

Appendix II was audited in canonical source order against repository main’s current pinned Mathlib revision db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d (v4.33.0). Project-source search found no project-owned replacement for these interfaces. The audit checked the category/functor core, standard concrete categories, full and faithful functors, natural transformations and equivalences, linear Yoneda/coyoneda, monoidal tensor functors, rigid duality, structured arrows, representable functors/elements, and the three universal-property examples. Current upstream was used only as a supplementary search surface after the pinned interfaces.

The determinant example in FC01-AII-U012 has an explicit pinned naturality equation, Matrix.GeneralLinearGroup.map_det, together with identity/composition laws for base-change maps on GL_n, but no audited declaration bundles these data as the textbook natural transformation GL_n(-) ⇒ (-)^×. That row is therefore recorded as an interface gap rather than inferred from the commuting square alone.
